- 2026-07-23 | user review | The VO detail shell fetched the full services collection for one known service, and the list built a root-relative detail URL without the app base path. | Fetch detail resources through their individual endpoint, and keep exported cross-app URL builders base-URL-aware while preserving scope as part of the identity.
- 2026-07-23 | self | Checked for list links after a fixed 700 ms wait and failed just before the asynchronous VO query populated the table. | Wait on a semantic row/footer condition for query-backed browser checks; if only fixed waits are available, inspect a fresh DOM snapshot before asserting a locator count.
- 2026-07-23 | self | The first unsupported-lock state paired “Lock information unavailable” with “No invocation currently holds the lock,” asserting absence after saying ownership could not be determined. | Treat unsupported, unlocked, and locked as three distinct UI states; never render an unlocked claim when the API cannot prove it.
- 2026-07-23 | self | Ran repo-root formatter paths from the query-library working directory and chained the test after it, so path resolution failed and suppressed the test. | Resolve command paths against the explicit tool working directory and run verification steps independently so one expected tooling failure cannot hide later checks.
- 2026-07-23 | self | I interpreted “review comments” as source-code commentary and added explanatory comments instead of reporting actionable review findings. | In review context, inspect the diff and emit inline `::code-comment` findings; only add source comments when the user clearly asks for comments inside the implementation.
- 2026-07-23 | user review | “Activity” obscured that the endpoint returns two independently bounded recent-entry sets, and the first implementation rebuilt invocation status while nesting the queue lookup inside the backlog query. | Name the contract “recent entries”; query shared/legacy invocations through `sys_invocation`; resolve the exact VO queue ID first, then point-read its entries so shared volume cannot consume the exclusive page.
- 2026-07-23 | user review | “Inbox” is the preferred VO concept, and compact VO rows should not introduce parallel status/handler UI when established invocation primitives already exist. | Name the endpoint and component Inbox; normalize compact invocation status with the shared status computation; let `Status` render status-only rows; and use `Target` so the handler segment retains its panel link.
- 2026-07-23 | user | A scoped Virtual Object can participate in multiple VQueues, one per limit key, so resolving a single queue loses exclusive work. | When `scoped_virtual_objects` is advertised, return flat exclusive entries with their source VQueue ID/status/limit key and group them only in the client; preserve the existing single-queue flat path when the feature is absent.
- 2026-07-23 | user review | Joined `sys_vqueues` to metadata while resolving scoped Virtual Object inboxes, obscuring the bounded queue selection and risking a broad entry-side scan. | First select a bounded set of matching metadata rows ordered by `num_inbox DESC`, then point-read `sys_vqueues` with those IDs and reattach queue metadata in memory.

- 2026-07-23 | user review | Eagerly hydrating every scoped VQueue made the instance response large and required increasingly complex lock-holder recovery and multi-queue SQL. | Let the instance endpoint discover only bounded non-empty VQueue metadata; load one VQueue's ordered inbox by ID only when it is displayed or expanded, and choose this presentation from the scoped identity rather than server capability alone.
- 2026-07-23 | user | Called scoped VQueues “lanes” in the API and UI, introducing a second name for an existing domain entity. | Use VQueue consistently in schema names, response properties, implementation identifiers, and user-facing copy.
- 2026-07-23 | user | Rendered a second `PanelTable` inside a spanning child cell for expanded VQueue entries, unlike the established state-object tree. | Use one `PanelTable` with a shared column model; emit VQueue inbox entries and loading/error/empty/truncation states as child `Row`s from `renderChildRows`.
- 2026-07-23 | user review | Added SQL sorting to VQueue inbox and discovery reads, and derived truncation after hydration dropped raced-away candidates. | Rely on the current physical VQueue inbox iterator order, leave bounded VQueue discovery unordered, and preserve the candidate query's 26th-row truncation sentinel even when hydration underfills the visible result.
- 2026-07-23 | user review | Described `sys_vqueues WHERE id = ...` as a point read and retained a redundant legacy `sys_inbox` sequence sort. | Describe the VQueue read accurately as a partition-narrowed inbox-stage scan whose storage iterator emits encoded queue order; omit sorting from both inbox sources so `LIMIT 26` can terminate early.

- 2026-07-23 | self review | I called `sys_vqueues WHERE id = ...` a point read even though the query table's ID extractor narrows only to the owning partition; the stage scanner still iterates that partition's selected stage and applies the ID filter above the scan. | Describe this as an exact-ID filtered partition/stage scan. Removing `ORDER BY` allows early cancellation after 26 matches, but does not turn it into an exact VQueue-prefix storage seek.

- 2026-07-24 | correction from user | I overstated the cap risk from broad scoped Inbox VQueue discovery: for one scoped VO identity, the exclusive lock permits at most one running/suspended/paused lock-holder entry, while an inboxed lock holder is already matched by `num_inbox > 0`. | The broad counter predicate is cheap and reasonable for best-effort visibility of the active VQueue. Its actual caveat is that unordered `LIMIT 26` followed by `slice(0, 25)` returns 25 VQueues total and does not guarantee the lock VQueue survives the cap; use separate lock-holder resolution only if that guarantee is required.
- 2026-07-24 | self review | Narrowing an HTTP query parameter with `as VirtualObjectInboxMode` removed runtime validation, and deleting the scoped-on-legacy guard made a scoped URL fall through to an unscoped legacy query. | Validate untrusted URL values at the router boundary before narrowing, and preserve capability checks that prevent one requested identity from returning another identity's data.
- 2026-07-24 | self review | Combining the lock and inbox into one HTTP response synchronized their React lifecycle, but the response still trusted a lock read taken before entry hydration; a handover can therefore produce a terminal old holder beside the new running entry. | Validate the hydrated holder (`has_lock` for VQueue-backed locks and non-terminal status), then conditionally reread and hydrate the lock once when validation fails. A shared response is not automatically a database snapshot.
- 2026-07-24 | self review | Inbox candidates can leave the inbox between discovery and entry-status hydration but remain in the returned page. | If the endpoint contract is strictly waiting inbox entries, filter VQueue-backed candidates by the hydrated `stage = 'inbox'` before the visible slice while retaining the discovery query's independent truncation sentinel.
- 2026-07-24 | self review | Tried to make scoped VQueue discovery race-safe by scanning/re-reading 250 candidate metadata rows and throwing when the refreshed lock did not match the initially hydrated holder. This remained non-transactional, made a stable lock VQueue outside the arbitrary cap fail permanently, and converted routine handovers into HTTP 500s. | Keep discovery at the response limit plus sentinel, validate and conditionally hydrate the refreshed holder, then exact-fetch its VQueue metadata if discovery omitted it. Never use a larger arbitrary cap or exceptions as a substitute for a snapshot guarantee.
- 2026-07-24 | user clarification | I treated client retry as an accidental consequence of a thrown server error, but the intended protocol is deliberately bounded optimistic snapshot assembly: read lock and candidates in parallel, hydrate/re-read in parallel, and ask the client to retry when reconciliation cannot prove consistency so the API never loops indefinitely. | Preserve the bounded protocol, but represent contention explicitly (for example a typed 409/retryable response), retry only that condition, and ensure phase-two hydration actually contains the fresh holder before claiming it can be reconciled. A one-time point hydration of the changed holder is bounded reconciliation, not an unbounded server retry loop.
- 2026-07-24 | pattern | The scoped VO implementation now point-hydrates a changed fresh holder, returns typed `409 snapshot_changed` when its VQueue cannot be reconciled, retries only that code once in the client, and exact-fetches a stable holder VQueue omitted from discovery. | Keep the candidate hydration cap as a measured retry-rate/latency tuning knob rather than a correctness boundary; apply the same explicit conflict result to non-scoped reconciliation instead of silently converting an invalid refreshed holder into “lock available.”

The new missing-holder test also used the impossible combination `stage = inbox` with `hasLock = true`. | Return/merge refreshed holder hydration together with the reconciled lock before filtering rows. Model races with valid state transitions (`inbox/hasLock=false` to `running/hasLock=true`) so tests exercise real VQueue invariants.
